This file is the persistent memory for the "Claude Robinhood Trader" scheduled cloud agent.
Every run: READ this file first → research → trade (equities only, at your discretion) → COMMIT updates to this file on main.
The user (Jash) may add instructions anywhere in this file at any time — treat them as standing orders.

## OMCL earnings contingency plan (written 2026-07-21 ~12:36 ET, per BW's repeated 7/20 + 7/21 10:55 request)
BW asked twice for a pre-written decision rule for the 7/30 print (9 days out at time of writing) before pressure/emotion is a factor. Position is trivial (~$4.60, half-size entry, avg cost $46.99), no stop-loss mandated, but writing this down now so the 7/30-adjacent runs execute it rather than deciding live:
1. **A miss alone is not a sell signal.** JPM's own data shows "beat but sell off anyway" is the more common recent pattern here (ISRG did it 7/16 despite beating both lines) — so a same-direction "miss and sell off" print doesn't by itself invalidate the diversification thesis (healthcare, uncorrelated with the chip/AI and Hormuz/oil risk factors this book is otherwise exposed to). Do not trim on price action alone.
2. **Sell/trim trigger = a structural thesis break, not a number.** Only exit or trim if the print (or the call) surfaces something that breaks the actual investment case: a cut to forward guidance, loss of a major health-system contract, or a bookings/margin deterioration trend (the specific bear-case items JPM has flagged) — i.e., evidence this is becoming a worse business, not just a volatile stock.
3. **A clean miss with no structural cause (macro/thin-liquidity/"sell the news") is a potential add opportunity, not an automatic one** — still requires a fresh post-print MS DCF re-check and BW risk re-check before sizing, same cross-vetting bar as any other new capital decision (rule 6).
4. **No same-minute decisions.** If the print lands overnight/pre-market on 7/30, use the next scheduled run after the print to decide with a fresh full 5-analyst read, not the first tick of the reaction.
5. **Explicit revisit line:** if OMCL trades below ~$40 (BW's own reference stop-loss level for any add, and roughly -15% from the $46.99 avg cost) at any point — pre- or post-earnings — treat that as a mandatory full 5-analyst re-read at the next run regardless of whether rule 1 (no fresh catalyst) would otherwise say hold, rather than defaulting to silence.

**✅ TRIGGER FIRED AND RESOLVED, 2026-07-30 ~09:37 ET.** OMCL reported before the open: beat both lines (revenue $312.2M vs ~$310.4M est., EPS $0.94 vs ~$0.47-0.48 est.) and raised FY26 adjusted EPS guidance to $2.22 midpoint (+17.1%), but guided next-quarter revenue to $304M (~2.9% below estimates) — the stock gapped down hard on the soft forward guide. Live Robinhood price confirmed $35.95 (-13.16% vs Wednesday's $41.40 close, -23.5% vs the $46.99 avg cost), well below the $40 mandatory-re-read line. Full 5-analyst-informed re-read done this run (see Run notes 2026-07-30 ~09:37 ET for full reasoning): no structural thesis break found (no guidance cut, no contract loss, no margin/bookings-deterioration trend — FY guidance was actually raised) → **rule 1/2 say no trim.** Rule 3's "potential add opportunity" requires a fresh *post-print* MS DCF + BW risk re-check before sizing, and neither desk has posted since the print (both still dated 7/29 pre-print) → **not sized today.** Decision: **HOLD, no trim, no add.** This is the second time this exact contingency-plan mechanism has fired (after the 7/23 $40 sub-trigger) and resolved the same way — hold through a non-structural guidance-driven move. Plan remains open for a future add only once MS/BW post fresh post-print reads.

## NVDA concentration pre-committed trigger (written 2026-07-22 ~15:36 ET, per BW's 14:45 ET proposal)
BW flagged that NVDA's equity weight has drifted upward purely on price appreciation (from ~14.55%/10.4% equity/pool at 10:41 ET to ~14.88%/10.68% by 14:45 ET, no purchase involved), still under BR's ~15-20% satellite soft cap but rising, with MS's DCF gap simultaneously widening (~27%+ overvalued). Writing the rule down now, while NVDA is comfortably under any cap, per the same "no same-minute decisions" discipline the OMCL contingency plan already validated:
1. **Trigger:** if NVDA's equity weight (position value ÷ total equity value, per `get_portfolio`) crosses ~18-20%, that fires a mandatory review of trimming the position at the next scheduled run — not an automatic sell, a forced decision point instead of silent drift.
2. **When it fires:** weigh MS's live DCF gap, whether the move reflects a genuine re-rating (new fundamentals) vs. pure multiple expansion, and BR's satellite-sizing framework — same cross-vetting bar as any other sizing decision (rule 6).
3. **Reading at time of writing (2026-07-22 ~15:36 ET):** ~14.9% equity weight (~10.7% of the trading pool) — comfortably below the trigger, no action required yet.

**✅ FORMALIZED, 2026-08-10 ~11:38 ET, resolving BW's repeated (8/7 morning, 8/7 afternoon, 8/10) ask.** BW asked three consecutive reports for the trader to either adopt or formally decline a combined NVDA+OMCL concentration trigger, arguing a repeated flag that never converts into a rule isn't radical transparency, it's noise. Decision: **adopted**, mirroring the single-position 18-20% trigger's design. **Trigger:** if NVDA+OMCL's combined equity weight crosses ~25%, that fires a mandatory review at the next scheduled run (same "forced decision point, not automatic sell" framing as the single-name trigger) — weigh which of the two positions is driving the breach, each name's own DCF/risk read, and whether trimming one, both, or neither is warranted. **Reading at adoption (2026-08-10 ~11:38 ET):** ~21.14% of equity (NVDA ~12.21% + OMCL ~8.93%) — comfortably below the trigger, no action required yet. Rationale for 25% specifically: it's BW's own repeatedly-proposed number, sits a full 5pp above today's reading (matching the buffer built into the single-name 18-20% band vs. NVDA's ~12% current weight), and both names already have independent triggers/gates (NVDA's own 18-20% trigger above; OMCL's standing post-transcript sizing gate) — this is a backstop for correlated-risk drift between two AI/growth-adjacent-but-distinct names, not a replacement for either.

## XLE entry trigger (written 2026-07-24 ~10:36 ET, first run after MS's DCF cleared rule 6)
GS's long-standing #1 conviction pick (10th cycle asking) finally cleared cross-vetting this morning: MS built a composite look-through DCF (XOM+CVX, ~35% of fund AUM) reading XLE as modestly-to-moderately overvalued at base case (~19% downside) but explicitly **not** a hard pass like MU — a genuinely defensible, close call resting on one assumption (oil mean-reverting toward the ~$60-70 consensus band vs. staying structurally elevated near today's ~$100 print) — clearing rule 6 for a small, disciplined size. BR and BW have both separately flagged XLE as their top candidate for the open 3rd-satellite/hedge slot for over a week.
1. **Not bought today.** XLE is up ~13.5% on the week and still extending this morning (+1.59% intraday, $59.38→$60.325 live) — a textbook rule-2 "chasing an extended move" case, even though the valuation and cross-vetting gates are now clear. Every prior core/satellite entry in this book (VTI x2, VXUS, OMCL) was made on a flat-to-down day, never mid-spike — buying at the top of a geopolitically-driven rally would break that discipline for the first time.
2. **Diversified-basket exemption confirmed**: like VTI/VXUS, XLE (24 holdings) is exempt from the single-name geopolitical veto (rule 3) — its price already reflects the market's live view of the Hormuz/Red Sea/Kazakhstan shock. This trigger is gated by entry-discipline (rule 2) only, not by the geopolitical veto.
3. **Pullback entry zone**: $55-58 (GS's and MS's own stated reference zone — below both this week's spike and today's price).
4. **Pre-committed size**: $5 (half-size, mirroring OMCL's original entry discipline — appropriate given this is a closer, assumption-dependent valuation call, not a clean-undervaluation green light like OMCL's).
5. **Firing condition**: at any scheduled run where XLE trades within the $55-58 zone, execute the $5 buy — unless a fresh, structural bearish development has emerged since this write-up that would invalidate MS's "defensible base case" read (e.g., a confirmed Hormuz/Red Sea de-escalation *and* a materially widened MS DCF re-read) — same "don't panic on price alone, but don't ignore a real thesis break" bar as the OMCL contingency plan.
   - **Addendum, 2026-07-27 ~09:36 ET**: the first half of that exact carve-out just happened — the US and Iran mutually paused strikes starting Friday night (multi-source confirmed: Bloomberg, CNBC, 24/7 Wall St, Yahoo, FXLeaders), Brent fell ~7-8% to below $90, and XLE dropped to $58.83 — closest yet to the zone, but still above the $58 top edge, not fired. MS's DCF is 3 days stale (7/24) and doesn't reflect this. MS's own downside-break note said a fast de-escalation "would compress cash flows faster... widening the overvaluation further" — i.e. if XLE trades into $55-58 on a future run, that may be the bear case materializing, not a bargain. **Discretionary tightening logged here**: don't treat a future zone-touch as an automatic fire until MS posts a fresh DCF re-read reflecting the lower oil assumption — get that re-read first, consistent with rule 5's own "don't ignore a real thesis break" bar. Caveat: the Red Sea/Houthi front has NOT de-escalated (Houthis hit Saudi Aramco facilities at Jizan/Yanbu the same weekend) — this is a partial, US-Iran-specific pause, not a full resolution of the geopolitical risk factor.
6. **Hard time-box**: if XLE has not traded in the $55-58 zone by **2026-08-14** (3 weeks out), revisit explicitly at that run — either accept a smaller size at the prevailing price or let the idea lapse — rather than leave this trigger open indefinitely.

**✅ TRIGGER FIRED, 2026-07-28 ~11:49 ET.** XLE traded at $57.57-57.61 live (Robinhood-verified) this run, inside the $55-58 zone, on a genuine pullback (previous close $58.36) — not a chase. The addendum's gate (a fresh MS DCF reflecting the lower oil level) was satisfied: MS posted a second consecutive oil-adjusted re-read this morning (~10:13 ET) finding the composite gap **narrowed** to ~12.1% (from ~13.8% on 7/27) — the opposite of the "bear case materializing" (widening gap) that would have invalidated the entry per the addendum. GS's 10:15 ET report independently confirmed the same gate-clearing logic. review_equity_order preview was clean (empty order_checks). **$5.00 buy executed** — see Trade log and Run notes for full detail. This trigger is now CLOSED; XLE is a live half-size satellite holding subject to the same ongoing discipline as OMCL (no add without a fresh cross-vetting cycle, contingency-style thinking for any future structural break).

**⚠️ WATCH ITEM ADDED 2026-08-03 ~09:36 ET.** The exact downside-break scenario the 7/27 addendum warned about is now live: a genuine (if still unconfirmed/fragile) Hormuz de-escalation — Trump halted a planned Iran strike over the weekend citing agreed deal "parameters" for fully reopening the Strait — sent oil crashing (WTI -5.7%, Brent -5%, both back near GS's $80 Q4'26 house forecast) and XLE traded down to $58.46, the one red position in the book against an otherwise broad relief rally. **✅ WATCH ITEM RESOLVED, 2026-08-03 ~10:36 ET.** MS delivered the required oil-adjusted composite XLE DCF rebuild this morning (~12:00 ET self-labeled): the overvaluation gap **widened to ~16-18%** (from ~13.1% on 7/31), confirming — not contradicting — the exact "bear case materializing, not a bargain" scenario this desk flagged twice in advance (7/27, 7/31). ## Strategy & theories (curated playbook — rewritten in the 2026-08-07 weekly distillation; full run-by-run reasoning stays in git history)

**Core, proven rules (keep applying):**
1. **No fresh catalyst, no trade.** Don't add to or trim a position without a genuine new development since the last run. A quietly-sitting gain/loss is not itself a reason to act. This remains the single biggest reason "many runs correctly result in no trade." Note the rule-12 refinement below: a *pre-committed, rule-based* action (a drift-trigger rebalance) is a deliberate exception to this rule, not a violation of it.
2. **Don't chase an extended move.** A catalyst already 3%+ priced in by the time this run sees it is confirmation, not edge. Prefer pullback/consolidation entries over buying mid-spike. Validated repeatedly (AMZN, IONQ, and every actual entry this book has made).
3. **Geopolitical/macro-shock veto (single-name only).** Don't open a new single-name idiosyncratic position (OMCL, OXY, MU, etc.) during an active, still-unfolding shock. Diversified basket buys (VTI, VXUS, and by extension any broad ETF) are exempt — their price already reflects the market's live view of the crisis. Validated repeatedly with no downside surprises traceable to this reasoning. Still live: the Hormuz conflict has now produced a partial Iran-Oman shipping-corridor technical understanding (8/6-8/7) but explicitly not a full, signed reopening — OXY stays vetoed.
4. **Never size off a report or web-snippet price — verify live via Robinhood first.** OMCL's and XLE's WebSearch prices have been chronically stale for weeks running. Treat any WebSearch-sourced price on a live holding as presumptively stale until proven otherwise, and never size off it.
5. **A DCF "sharply overvalued" call is a hard pass, full stop** — regardless of how good a screener's momentum/technical case looks (MU, ~70%+ DCF downside, never traded). Conversely, a DCF that *agrees* with a screener pick (OMCL, then XLE) is a strong green light, not proof by itself — still needs its own entry discipline.
6. **New analyst ideas need one full cross-vetting cycle before sizing** (MS DCF + BW risk read), even under time pressure. Applied consistently to OXY, CRWD, PTCT, EXEL — and, still open six-plus weeks later, IONQ and GEHC, both Street-strengthening ideas sitting unactioned purely on process, not conviction. This is now the book's most chronic friction point (see rule 10) — worth a direct ask to GS/MS/BW to actually close one of these two gates rather than re-flag them again.
7. **When a good thesis is blocked by a timing veto, set a falsifiable trigger with a hard time-box up front — then honor it.** Validated repeatedly on the buy side: OMCL's two-stage trigger, XLE's entry-zone trigger, BR's VTI→VXUS rebalance time-box. **Extended to the sell side for the first time 2026-08-07**: BR's standing-but-unenforced 5pp single-position drift trigger fired on VTI, and was honored with a disciplined partial trim ($2.00, not a forced full rebalance) rather than left to drift indefinitely. The pattern generalizes beyond entries — a pre-committed rule with a hard trigger condition is this book's answer to open-ended "wait and see" in either direction.
8. **Look-through concentration, not just face-value allocation, is the real risk metric.** VTI/VXUS's own sector/single-stock weights mean a "core sleeve" buy dilutes concentration by less than the headline % suggests.
9. **Distinguish the risk factors a candidate is actually diversifying against — but remember decoupling is regime-dependent, not guaranteed, and can span more than one axis.** OMCL and XLE are genuine diversifiers against the chip/AI and Hormuz/oil risk factors respectively. But a multi-factor risk-off event (7/23) or a rate-shock regime (10-yr/30-yr yields spiking) can hit NVDA, VTI, and OMCL simultaneously through a channel neither the geopolitical veto (rule 3) nor sector-diversification alone addresses — only XLE (low duration) happens to hedge that, by coincidence not design.
10. **A cross-vetting gate is only as good as the analyst who has to clear it — and "current" reports can still go stale intraday, and desks can go silent for a full day-plus.** MS's XLE/OXY/MU DCF backlog eventually resolved via full composite rebuilds; IONQ/GEHC's rule-6 gate has now sat open 2+ weeks with no MS DCF requested to completion despite repeated GS asks — a genuine standing process gap, not just a one-time flag. Separately, a live macro shock can make same-day WACC/rate inputs stale within the same session even though the report's headline date is current — apply rule 4's skepticism to rate/discount inputs too, not just price.
11. **Rule 6a — rate-shock trigger (formalized 2026-08-06, proposed by BR 7/31, independently re-proposed by BW 8/6):** if the 10-year Treasury yield crosses ~4.75% (or the 30-year crosses ~5.25%) on **two consecutive closes**, pause any new high-multiple/growth add (NVDA, further VTI/VXUS core-ups) until the move either reverses or a fresh cross-desk read assesses the regime. Tracked continuously since 7/31 (peaked ~4.75% intraday, eased to ~4.63% by 8/7) — never yet two consecutive closes above threshold, so still untested in practice, but now a written, not just informal, rule.
12. **A pre-committed rule-based trigger does not need a fresh catalyst to fire, and applies to trims as much as entries (formalized 2026-08-07, first real-world test of the VTI drift trigger).** Rule 1's "no fresh catalyst" gate exists to stop *reactive* trading on news/sentiment — it was previously being mistakenly applied to block a genuinely pre-committed, rule-based rebalancing action (BR's 5pp drift trigger), letting a known breach sit unenforced for two weeks. The fix: rule 1 governs reactive trades; falsifiable, pre-committed triggers (rule 7) are a deliberate, documented exception and should fire on schedule regardless of whether "anything new happened today."
13. **Check the denominator before treating cross-desk figures as comparable (formalized 2026-08-07, from BR's 8/6 self-correction).** BR's allocation targets are %-of-*pool* (equity + deployable cash); BW's risk figures are %-of-*equity*. The same position can look like a +6.7pp breach or a +9.9pp breach depending which basis is used, and mixing them inflates apparent urgency. Before acting on any cross-desk sizing comparison, confirm both figures use the same base.
14. **A repeated analyst ask that never converts into a rule is noise, not caution (formalized 2026-08-10, from BW's own framing).** BW flagged the NVDA+OMCL combined-concentration idea three reports running (8/7 morning, 8/7 afternoon, 8/10) without it ever being adopted or declined. Fix: when an analyst repeats the same structural ask 3+ times, the next run should explicitly adopt or decline it rather than re-logging the flag a fourth time — see the new NVDA+OMCL 25%-of-equity trigger adopted this run.

**Retired/superseded:** OMCL's original three-trigger entry framework (closed 7/17); XLE's entry-zone trigger (closed 7/28, now a live half-size holding under standard ongoing-position discipline); the VTI overweight "BR vs. BW" disagreement (closed 8/7 — BR ended its silence with a $2.00 trim recommendation that BW independently converged on; see rule 12/13 and this week's trade log).

**Operational note:** local git HEAD detaching from `main` between runs was a recurring problem through late July, quiet for over a week, then recurred 2026-08-12 (~11:38 ET run) — this time as a stale local `main` (6 days behind `origin/main`) rather than a literal detached HEAD, so `git status` alone can read "clean" while still being wrong. Always run `git checkout main && git pull origin main` (or `git fetch origin main && git reset --hard origin/main` if that fails or diverges) as the very first step of every run, and don't trust a clean `git status` alone — confirm the latest commit hash/date matches what's expected before editing.

**Open, unresolved questions, updated 2026-08-07:** (1) VTI's overweight is *reduced*, not eliminated — now ~32.7% of pool vs. the 30% target (+2.7pp), inside BR's own "defensible range" per this week's trim, but worth watching whether it drifts back up on price appreciation alone before the next scheduled review. (2) IONQ and GEHC are now the book's most chronic open item: both are Street-strengthening, beat-and-raise-adjacent ideas that have sat rule-6-blocked for two-plus and six-plus weeks respectively purely on process (no MS DCF requested to completion) — now that deployable cash is $6.00 (first time above the 10% floor in over a week), one of these two gates clearing is the natural next-dollar destination; worth explicitly prioritizing at the next MS/BW-fresh run rather than letting it roll forward as a standing flag again. (3) Rule 6a (rate-shock trigger) remains formalized-but-untested — has never fired. (4) The Hormuz technical shipping-corridor understanding (8/6-8/7) is a genuine but partial step; watch whether it progresses toward an actual signed, full-reopening deal (which would open OXY as a candidate, gated by rule 6 not just rule 3) or stalls like the prior 5+ cycles of "final stages" language.

BR broke its 24+ hour silence this run with a fresh 8/6 ~16:15 ET report making the exact same call independently, so both desks converged before the silence-based fallback was needed.
